- **CARRYING THE BOX WITH THREE ATTACHED IS UNIVERSAL (d16f TOO), AND THE LIFT IS UNEVENLY SHARED (-6d, 2026-10-27 ~01:45; diag aeee5d6 PM-reviewed; both evals reproduce their earlier rows exactly). (1) HOOK WEIGHT, pinned in the env: _hook_force is the pin spring force on the hook (construction.py:281, kp (attach - hook) - kd dv), self.tension = |pin| (line 286); quasi-static, pin = wire pull minus the hook's weight, so the force on the box is T_wire + m_h g per drone and up_over_mg is NET of 3 x 0.30 kg (hook_mass, construction_cfg.py:84): at d16q 18k sum F_up = 1.04 m g means the wires' vertical sum is ~19 N ~ 1.94 m g, ~6.3 N per drone. PM corollary: D19's f_safe = 0.3 x 1 kg x g = 2.94 N = exactly one hook's weight, so the per-drone cap (|pin| <= 2.94 N) is REACHABLE and means 'each drone's load on the box between -2.94 and +2.94 N'; three drones at the cap can still unload 0.9 m g, so the cap bounds no team quantity. (2) PER-DRONE SPLIT (d16q 18k vs d16f it_59999 jh0): all-three steps / risk < 1 653 / 0.015 vs 8120 / 0.075; up_over_mg q10/50/90 0.64 / 1.04 / 1.55 vs 0.17 / 0.88 / 1.64; up per holder N (net of hook) -0.6 / 4.3 / 6.0 vs -2.2 / 3.5 / 6.4; up max share 0.38 / 0.52 / 0.84 vs 0.40 / 0.59 / 1.34 (> 1 = one drone's net up negative, its wire pulls less than its hook weighs); per-holder horizontal q50 3.7 vs 4.7; odd-drone horizontal q50 3.9 vs 6.5; net_h q50 5.0 vs 7.4; net / odd 1.12 vs 1.29; dominant tip / slide / lift 57 / 586 / 0 vs 28 / 7484 / 0. READ: d16f also carries most of the box with three attached (median 0.88 m g): not a d16q trait; d16f's wider spread (q10 0.17) is why it has 7.5 % safe all-three steps against d16q's 1.5 % (the safe steps are the low-sum-F_up ones). The lift is not shared: the top drone carries 52-59 % at the median and one drone often sags below its hook's weight. d16q's 2.5 per N force penalty cut HORIZONTAL pull (odd drone 3.9 vs 6.5 N) but not vertical: sum F_up is higher in d16q than in d16f. Two checkpoints of two runs agree on the quantity; d16q 20k-24k and d16p 46k+ rows carry all three fields and give the ladder.**
- **MEASURED: WITH ALL THREE ATTACHED THE DRONES CARRY THE BOX (sum F_up / m g q50 1.04); THE ALL-THREE WALL IS UNLOADING, NOT THE MU GAP (-6d, 2026-10-27 ~01:00; diag 9b2a1b3 PM-reviewed; d16q it_18000, 64 x 8000, identical except vbox_mu; rows in -6d's scratchpad mu_cmp.jsonl, not the tracker files): mu 0.6 (contract) vs mu 0.8 (training): catches / held / detaches 177 / 0.898 / 29 both; exactly-two risk > 1 0.832 vs 0.791; safe >= 1 s spells 6 vs 9; protocol 0 both (protocol_run 0.03 s n 4); all-attached risk < 1 0.015 vs 0.017; dominant term tip / slide / lift 57 / 586 / 0 vs 86 / 556 / 0. up_over_mg q10/50/90 0.64 / 1.04 / 1.55 (mu-independent): on the median all-three step the drones pull up harder than the box weighs, the normal force sits at the 5 % floor (construction.py:412), slide = |F_h| / (mu x 0.05 m g), so any horizontal net slides it at either mu; lift ~1.04 is also > 1 but slide has the larger ratio. The earlier 'per-holder modest' read is corrected: horizontally modest (3.7 N), vertically the three together lift about the box weight, so the box has no friction budget left. The light-hold target for three is VERTICAL: total upward pull well below m g (e.g. <= 0.5 m g leaves ~2.9 N of slide budget at mu 0.6). Hypothesis for the user, not a lever (one checkpoint). PM ARITHMETIC: hold_safe_force_frac 0.3 caps each drone at 0.3 m g, so three drones at the cap pulling near-vertically already unload 0.9 m g: a per-drone cap tuned for one holder cannot bound the team's unloading; the team quantity is sum F_up. Whether _hook_force includes the hooks' own weight (3 x 0.30 kg = 0.9 m g of load on a 1 kg box if it does; the vbox comment at construction.py:385 says the vbox force does) decides the numbers and is asked of -6d. d16p 46k's row predates the field; rows from now on carry it.**
- **ALL-ATTACHED FORCE PICTURE AT d16q 18k: THREE PULLS FAIL BY SLIDING, AND THE ODD DRONE ALONE SETS THE NET FORCE (-6d, 2026-10-27 ~00:15; diag 937ac4c, PM-reviewed; ERRATUM: the tracker's _3drone rows are 64 envs x 8000 steps, not 128 x 3000 as labelled in the rows above; every row sent was the tracker's own, only the label was wrong; the committed diag reproduces the tracker row exactly at 64 x 8000): all-attached steps 653, horizontal force on the box q10/50/90 N: per holder 2.7 / 3.7 / 5.9; net 1.7 / 5.0 / 13.3; cancellation 0.19 / 0.42 / 0.81; one odd drone on 653 / 653 steps (three drones on three of four faces always leave exactly one with no holder opposite); odd pull 2.9 / 3.9 / 5.8; opposite-pair residual 0.9 / 2.3 / 8.7; net / odd 0.58 / 1.12 / 2.9; dominant vbox term on risk > 1 steps slide 586, tip 57, lift 0. READ: net ~ the odd drone's pull (net / odd q50 1.12) while the pair cancels to ~2.3 N, and per-holder horizontal pull is already modest (3.7 N): the all-three state fails by the odd drone, not by magnitude. PM CHECK OF THE PLANT (construction.py:414): slide = |sum F_h| / (vbox_mu x (m g - F_up)), normal force floored at 5 % of m g. The tracker's eval cfg (r0_3drone/run.sh) sets vbox_mu 0.6, pin_kp 150, box_size_dr, bar_band_random, bar_lateral_frac 0.6 = the CONTRACT plant, while d16f / d16p / d16q TRAIN at the cfg defaults vbox_mu 0.8, pin_kp 400, no box DR, bar_lateral_frac 1.0 (R0 geometry, decision d16f). So the slide budget with the box unloaded is 7.8 N in training and 5.9 N in evaluation: the policy is taught a looser limit than the one it is scored on, and a net of 5.0 N is inside the training budget with little unloading but over the eval budget once F_up exceeds ~1.5 N (15 % of the weight). -6d's inference of 'substantial upward unloading' is therefore partly the mu gap; the field does not record sum F_up, a one-line follow-up (sum F_up / m g) is requested, plus a one-off re-eval of d16q 18k at vbox_mu 0.8 to measure how much of the 98.5 % risk > 1 is the plant gap. HYPOTHESIS (-6d, not a lever, one row): a light three-drone hold needs a near-vertical odd pull or a box left with its weight; under D18 the odd drone can know it is odd only from the plan (assigned faces are plan geometry). This bears on the open D17 geometry split (§4).**

- **PROTOCOL COUNTER vs JOINT-HOLD TERMINATION: NO OFF-BY-ONE, BUT THE EVAL MAKES THE PROTOCOL ONE-SHOT (-6d, 2026-10-26 ~09:15): step ordering (DirectMARLEnv step -> _get_dones -> _get_rewards -> reset, attach_update_in_dones true): _get_dones updates attached, _get_rewards increments _joint_hold on that state, the diag's post-step attached is the same state, so both count from the same first all-attached step; when _joint_hold reaches 100 at step t-1, step t terminates, and steps t-100..t-1 were all live, so ok can be true on all 100 and proto_run reaches 100 before the reset is applied. Empirical: longest_all_attached_s max is exactly 1.0 s in every late row (d16f 52k-60k, d16p 22k/24k) = 100 live steps; the env's own all-attached joint hold (joint_hold_light false in eval) is 5.3 % of episodes at d16f 60k, 0.7-1.5 % at 52k-58k, 0.5-0.7 % at d16p 22k/24k. CONSEQUENCE: with joint_hold_light false the eval ends the episode after its first 100 consecutive all-attached steps whether or not taut + risk < 1 held, so a protocol success needs that first second clean on all 100 steps, no second attempt: at d16f 60k ~5 % of episodes had a full second of three attached and none passed taut + risk < 1 throughout. -6d: a property of the eval cfg, not a bug; keep the cfg and measure how close the misses are (new field protocol_run_s_nonzero_q50_90_max_count = longest protocol run per episode over episodes that had one; in the same uncommitted diag diff, now +18/-6; the 56k re-eval carries it, 54k does not). PM: agreed on the mechanism and the field, but a termination stricter than the protocol's definition cannot remain the protocol's measurement condition -> D21 (§4), protocol v1.3 proposed, one diagnostic row requested.**

Budget: a 1 kg box at vbox_mu 0.6 resists ~0.6 x (9.81 - 3.2) ~ 4.0 N horizontally; the median net horizontal ~9.2 N is ~2.3x the budget, matching risk 2-3, so the pair's net outward pull must drop by more than half. PM geometry note: three drones on four faces always include adjacent pairs (two of the three pairs), and with two attached the pair is adjacent 2 / 3 of the time; an adjacent pair's outward components add at 90 deg, so even perfect lateral cancellation leaves ~sqrt(2) x each holder's outward pull: to stay inside 4.0 N each holder may pull outward at most ~2.8 N ~ 0.29 x weight, i.e. a pull target near 0.3, not 0.4, OR holders wait with near-vertical wires (zero horizontal) until all three are attached, which is D5 option (a) (penalise the horizontal pull / reward wire verticality while not all attached); a pull term shaped by the team's NET horizontal force on the box is reward-side only and D18-compatible (D18 constrains observations, not training rewards). vbox_terminate_from later only delays the same wall. -6d offers a face-pair split per spell in the diag; PM asked for it (adjacent vs opposite pairs, risk and net outward per class) to size the pull target for the next arm. CORRECTION (-6d): the two-attached force fields are PER HOLDER, not net: outward q50 9.0 N is EACH holder's outward force, so an adjacent pair nets ~9 x sqrt(2) ~ 12.7 N against the ~4.0 N budget, about 3x (not 2.3x); cancellation = |sum F_h| / sum |F_h| (1 none, 0.71 equal perpendicular pulls), q50 0.75 fits mostly adjacent pairs. Sizing stands: each holder <= ~2.8 N outward, pull target ~0.3. Face-pair split added to tests/diag_seq_hook.py (uncommitted): every exactly-two step classed adjacent / opposite / same_face with steps and q10 / 50 / 90 of net horizontal force, cancellation and risk; tested and COMMITTED locally as flycrane-meta 49e3f64 (diag only, PM review: no defect, row in code_quality_control.md); in every tracker row from now on. d16f it_32000, 64 x 3000, off, 43 catches: ADJACENT 10565 steps, net horizontal on the box q10 / 50 / 90 10.8 / 14.0 / 15.1 N, cancellation 0.65 / 0.75 / 0.80, risk 3.0 / 3.0 / 3.0 (saturated); OPPOSITE 0 steps; same_face 0. So every two-holder state in this sample is an adjacent pair at ~3.5x the ~4.0 N slide budget. No opposite pair formed against the 1 / 3 expectation for random first catchers (p ~ 0.06 at ~7 spells): either the first two catches favour adjacent faces or the assignment seldom puts two early catchers opposite; small sample, to be watched on the full rows. PM code check (construction.py reset / _spawn): assigned faces are a fresh randperm(4)[:3] per env, so every env holds exactly one opposite pair and no face is structurally special (faces 0 / 1 = +-x, 2 / 3 = +-y, matching the diag's classes); catches by face were even at 18k (21 / 17 / 18 / 18), so the zero-opposite result is either a behavioural pattern of the first two catchers or sampling at ~7 spells (p ~ 0.06); not a plan-geometry effect. RESOLVED on the full 128 x 3000 row at 34k: opposite pairs are 36 % of two-attached steps (13495 vs adjacent 24472), so the 1 / 3 expectation holds and the 64-env zero was sampling. Adjacent: net horizontal q10 / 50 / 90 4.3 / 12.5 / 17.4 N, cancellation 0.53 / 0.80 / 0.96, risk 0.87 / 3.0 / 3.0. Opposite: net 3.1 / 6.8 / 9.1 N, cancellation 0.17 / 0.46 / 0.62, risk 1.10 / 2.55 / 3.0. EVEN OPPOSITE PAIRS exceed the ~4 N budget because the two holders pull UNEQUALLY (cancellation 0.46, not ~0), so 'pull gently' (D19) is needed for both pair types, not an adjacent-only fix. Sizing stands: each holder's outward pull <= ~2.8 N (~0.29 W) for an adjacent pair, or near-zero horizontal while not all attached (D5 (a)). -6d stages the cfg diffs for both options after the 44k read. - **Pose-free Hebbian question, pre-registered framing (-0f, 2026-10-20):** can online Hebbian plasticity on a frozen drone-only MAPPO body produce heading control (protocol transport success) when the basket pose is never observed? Information status: the pose IS recoverable from the drone-only stream (ego-frame estimator 1-2 cm / 2-4 deg within 0.5-3 s, ident_probe 2026-10-10; non-linear decoder ~10 deg heading over the episode, probe 3221624), so the test is about the plastic head's capacity and credit assignment, not identifiability (the old 25 vs 23 deg number was estimator-limited). Conditions (hover_rand, common_env TASK, eval seeds 123 / 124 / 125, 1024 envs, curricula off, deterministic): C0 ceiling with pose = paper-obs teacher 88-90 %, ori 11-12 deg (3224522); C1 frozen head = ABCD zero, warm start v12 it 2300: 6.7-7.7 %, ori 89-90 deg (3223853); C1' frozen head with basket_init_pose (mappo_v13_basket best, expected ~C1); P1 plastic warm start + rules from zero + curriculum = es17 headx4 / teacher final (3223909, ~2026-10-21 15:00; gen-7 snapshot 1.3-1.9 %, ori 101-106 deg, below C1); P2 rules N(0, 0.03), no curriculum = es18_th003 (~2026-10-21 23:00); P3 = P2 on basket_init_pose = es19 (~2026-10-22 06:30); P0 negative control = es18_p512r8 (0.3 destroys the warm start); **new H3 pair: C1-H3 frozen (mappo_v14_h3) vs P4 plastic H3 (es20_h3)**. Primary metric: protocol success + final ori, each condition both plastic and with hebbian_lr 0 on the same theta (isolates plasticity from head drift), same seeds. Readout: 'Hebbian resolves it' = a plastic condition beats C1 by >= 10 points protocol AND >= 20 deg lower final ori on all three seeds, with its lr-0 twin at C1 level; practical heading control = ori <= 45 deg and protocol >= 40 %; 'cannot without pose at this budget' = every plastic condition within +-5 points / +-10 deg of C1; in between = partial, reported with numbers. Training-time ori curves are not a readout. What the runs cannot answer: whether a plastic head COULD use the recoverable pose if credit assignment were easier (all evolve 4 x (33+12 -> 32 -> 5) ABCD rules, 100-step update interval, under chaotic pair-difference noise); a null = 'not with this head, interval and ES budget'. **Cheapest capacity probe (for the user, not launched): supervised upper bound on the same head: frozen body, plastic head online, rules fitted against the paper-obs teacher's actions (teach_mse, TW large, no task reward; --teacher_weight exists), 1 V100 x 12 h; if the head cannot imitate the pose-aware teacher below the frozen head's error, capacity is the blocker (next variables: recurrent / longer interval / 3-frame input); if it can, the ES signal is the blocker (denser heading fitness).**

The only transport policy that sees the live basket pose and controls heading is the paper-obs MAPPO teacher teacher_paperobs_best_1018.pt (45 x 3 frames incl. basket pose: paper 464, survival 0.92, ori 19 deg; no protocol-success number yet; -0f can run a 1 h eval on the user's say-so). **Protocol eval of that teacher (3224522, user-approved): transport success 88.2 / 90.4 / 90.3 % over eval seeds 123 / 124 / 125** (1024 envs, hover_rand plant, curricula and success bonus off, deterministic; final pos 0.09-0.11 m, final ori 11-12 deg; protocol success equals survival within 1-2 points on every seed (88/87, 90/90, 90/90), so the teacher's only failure mode on this plant is the ~10 % crashes, not precision, and the three seeds agree within 2 points, so one eval seed suffices for future comparisons; correction: paper obs set, 45 dims single frame incl. the live basket pose). Same protocol: drone-only MAPPO warm start 6.7-7.7 %, v17 plastic 1.3-1.9 %.
